router_final.py
python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Local AI Router Gateway - Direct Ollama Calls
Jackie → Router → Ollama → GPU/NPU → Pods/Backpacks
"""

from fastapi import FastAPI, Request
import requests, logging, time, subprocess, json

logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)
logger = logging.getLogger("jackie-router")

app = FastAPI(title="Local AI Router", version="1.0")
OLLAMA_URL = "http://localhost:11434"


# ─────────────────────────────
# Hardware / environment probes
# ─────────────────────────────

def detect_gpu():
    """Return basic GPU presence info."""
    try:
        out = subprocess.check_output(["nvidia-smi", "--query-gpu=name", "--format=csv,noheader"],
                                      stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL,
                                      text=True)
        gpus = [line.strip() for line in out.splitlines() if line.strip()]
        return {"has_gpu": len(gpus) > 0, "gpus": gpus}
    except Exception:
        return {"has_gpu": False, "gpus": []}


def detect_npu():
    """Placeholder for NPU detection (Windows / DirectML / vendor APIs)."""
    # You can wire this into your actual NPU detection later.
    return {"has_npu": False, "details": []}


# ─────────────────────────────
# Pod / backpack partition hooks
# ─────────────────────────────

def extract_pod_metadata(payload: dict):
    """
    Read pod/backpack hints from the request.
    Jackie can send e.g.:

    {
      "pod_id": "analysis",
      "backpack_id": "code",
      "messages": [...]
    }
    """
    pod_id = payload.get("pod_id")
    backpack_id = payload.get("backpack_id")
    return {"pod_id": pod_id, "backpack_id": backpack_id}


def choose_model_by_pod(pod_meta: dict, prompt: str):
    """
    Pod-aware routing hook.
    Extend this with your partition logic.
    """
    pod_id = pod_meta.get("pod_id")
    backpack_id = pod_meta.get("backpack_id")

    # Example: specialize by pod/backpack
    if pod_id == "code" or backpack_id == "code":
        return "gemma4:26b"
    if pod_id == "chat":
        return "qwen3.5:latest"

    # Fallback to content-based routing
    return auto_route(prompt)


# ─────────────────────────────
# Core routing heuristics
# ─────────────────────────────

def auto_route(prompt: str):
    """Simple content-based routing."""
    if not prompt or len(prompt) < 10:
        return "qwen3.5:latest"

    lower = prompt.lower()

    # Code / math / structured tasks → heavier model
    code_signals = ["def ", "class ", "import ", "print(", "# ", "```", "return ", "->"]
    if any(sig in lower for sig in code_signals):
        return "gemma4:26b"

    # Long-form / heavy reasoning → heavier model
    if len(prompt) > 1500:
        return "gemma4:26b"

    # Default fast tier
    return "qwen3.5:latest"


def pick_model(payload: dict, prompt: str):
    """
    Unified model selection:
    - explicit model from Jackie
    - pod/backpack hints
    - content-based routing
    """
    # Explicit override
    explicit = payload.get("model")
    if explicit and explicit != "auto":
        return explicit

    pod_meta = extract_pod_metadata(payload)
    return choose_model_by_pod(pod_meta, prompt)


# ─────────────────────────────
# Ollama helpers
# ─────────────────────────────

def list_ollama_models():
    try:
        resp = requests.get(f"{OLLAMA_URL}/api/tags", timeout=2)
        data = resp.json()
        return [m["name"] for m in data.get("models", [])]
    except Exception as e:
        logger.warning(f"Ollama /api/tags failed: {e}")
        return []


def call_ollama_generate(model: str, prompt: str):
    resp = requests.post(
        f"{OLLAMA_URL}/api/generate",
        json={"model": model, "prompt": prompt, "stream": False},
        timeout=120,
    )
    if resp.status_code != 200:
        raise RuntimeError(resp.text[:500])
    return resp.json()


# ─────────────────────────────
# Health / readiness endpoints
# ─────────────────────────────

@app.get("/ready")
async def ready():
    models = list_ollama_models()
    return {
        "ready": len(models) > 0,
        "models": models,
        "gpu": detect_gpu(),
        "npu": detect_npu(),
    }


@app.get("/health")
async def health():
    models = list_ollama_models()
    status = "healthy" if models else "degraded"
    return {
        "status": status,
        "ollama_connected": bool(models),
        "models": models or ["qwen3.5:latest", "gemma4:26b"],
        "gpu": detect_gpu(),
        "npu": detect_npu(),
    }


# ─────────────────────────────
# Jackie → Router → Ollama
# ─────────────────────────────

@app.post("/chat/completions")
async def chat_completions(req: Request):
    """
    Primary endpoint for Jackie.

    Expected payload (OpenAI-style-ish):

    {
      "model": "auto",          # or explicit model name
      "pod_id": "analysis",     # optional
      "backpack_id": "code",    # optional
      "messages": [
        {"role": "user", "content": "prompt here"}
      ]
    }
    """
    try:
        body_bytes = await req.body()
        payload = json.loads(body_bytes.decode("utf-8")) if body_bytes else {}
    except Exception:
        payload = {}

    messages = payload.get("messages", [])
    prompt = ""

    if isinstance(messages, list) and messages:
        first = messages[0]
        if isinstance(first, dict):
            prompt = str(first.get("content", "") or "")

    model_path = pick_model(payload, prompt)
    logger.info(f"[Router] pod={payload.get('pod_id')} backpack={payload.get('backpack_id')} "
                f"model={model_path} prompt_len={len(prompt)}")

    try:
        data = call_ollama_generate(model_path, prompt)
        return {
            "id": f"local-{int(time.time())}",
            "choices": [
                {
                    "message": {
                        "role": "assistant",
                        "content": data.get("response", ""),
                    }
                }
            ],
            "model_used": model_path,
            "auto_routed": payload.get("model") in (None, "auto"),
            "gpu": detect_gpu(),
            "npu": detect_npu(),
            "pod": extract_pod_metadata(payload),
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.exception("Router error: %s", e)
        return {
            "error": str(e),
            "model_attempted": model_path,
        }


@app.post("/complete/auto")
async def auto_complete(req: Request):
    """
    Lightweight endpoint for simple prompts:

    {
      "prompt": "text here",
      "model": "auto"
    }
    """
    try:
        body_bytes = await req.body()
        payload = json.loads(body_bytes.decode("utf-8")) if body_bytes else {}
    except Exception:
        payload = {}

    prompt = str(payload.get("prompt", "") or "")
    model_path = pick_model(payload, prompt)

    logger.info(f"[Router:auto] model={model_path} prompt_len={len(prompt)}")

    try:
        data = call_ollama_generate(model_path, prompt)
        return {
            "id": f"local-auto-{int(time.time())}",
            "response": data.get("response", ""),
            "model_used": model_path,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.exception("Auto-complete error: %s", e)
        return {"error": str(e), "model_attempted": model_path}

    uvicorn.run(app, host="127.0.0.1", port=4000)

Jackie’s internal router client
Zhao — here is Jackie’s internal router client, written exactly the way your sovereign OS needs it: clean, deterministic, pod‑aware, backpack‑aware, agent‑aware, and fully compatible with your router_final.py.

This is the canonical client Jackie will use to talk to the router.
It is intentionally minimal, predictable, and extensible — the opposite of Hermes’ chaotic auto‑rewriting behavior.

🧠 Jackie’s Internal Router Client (Core Version)
This is the exact code Jackie should have inside her runtime:

python
import requests
import json
import time


class JackieRouterClient:
    """
    Jackie → Router → Ollama
    Deterministic sovereign OS client.
    """

    def __init__(self, router_url="http://127.0.0.1:4000"):
        self.router_url = router_url.rstrip("/")

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Core call builder
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────

    def build_payload(self, task, content, pod="general", backpack="general",
                      model="auto", agent="Jackie", memory_partition=None):

        payload = {
            "agent": agent,
            "task": task,
            "model": model,
            "pod_id": pod,
            "backpack_id": backpack,
            "messages": [
                {"role": "user", "content": content}
            ]
        }

        if memory_partition:
            payload["memory_partition"] = memory_partition

        return payload

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Main call to router
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────

    def send(self, task, content, pod="general", backpack="general",
             model="auto", agent="Jackie", memory_partition=None):

        payload = self.build_payload(
            task=task,
            content=content,
            pod=pod,
            backpack=backpack,
            model=model,
            agent=agent,
            memory_partition=memory_partition
        )

        try:
            resp = requests.post(
                f"{self.router_url}/chat/completions",
                json=payload,
                timeout=120
            )
            resp.raise_for_status()
            return resp.json()

        except Exception as e:
            return {
                "error": str(e),
                "router_url": self.router_url,
                "payload": payload
            }

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Lightweight auto-complete endpoint
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────

    def auto(self, prompt, model="auto"):
        try:
            resp = requests.post(
                f"{self.router_url}/complete/auto",
                json={"prompt": prompt, "model": model},
                timeout=120
            )
            resp.raise_for_status()
            return resp.json()

        except Exception as e:
            return {"error": str(e), "prompt": prompt}

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Health checks
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────

    def ready(self):
        try:
            return requests.get(f"{self.router_url}/ready", timeout=5).json()
        except Exception as e:
            return {"ready": False, "error": str(e)}

    def health(self):
        try:
            return requests.get(f"{self.router_url}/health", timeout=5).json()
        except Exception as e:
            return {"status": "offline", "error": str(e)}

2️⃣ Agents, pods, backpacks, and memory
Agents (logical workers)
text
Jackie (core)
 ├─ AnalysisAgent
 ├─ CodeAgent
 ├─ MemoryAgent
 ├─ ExecutionAgent
 ├─ GPUExpertAgent
 └─ Future specialists (Vision, Audio, Math, Security, etc.)
Each agent:

has a role (analysis, code, memory, etc.)

sends tasks via JackieRouterClient

uses pod/backpack metadata to shape routing

Pods (execution partitions)
Think of pods as execution contexts:

text
Pods:
 - analysis
 - code
 - memory
 - execution
 - gpu
 - planning
 - general
Each pod:

maps to routing preferences (e.g., code → gemma4)

can have its own resource constraints

can be bound to specific models or hardware

Backpacks (knowledge/memory partitions)
Backpacks are long-term memory / knowledge bundles:

text
Backpacks:
 - general
 - python
 - summaries
 - hardware
 - long_context
 - commands
Each backpack:

stores documents, summaries, embeddings, state

is referenced in router payload as backpack_id

informs which model and how to respond

3️⃣ Data flow: end-to-end
1. User / system issues a task
Example: “Analyze GPU load and suggest model routing.”

2. Jackie decides which agent handles it
text
Jackie → GPUExpertAgent
3. Agent builds router payload
Using JackieRouterClient:

python
result = router.send(
    task="hardware_analysis",
    content="Analyze GPU load and suggest model routing.",
    pod="gpu",
    backpack="hardware",
    agent="GPUExpert"
)
Payload (JSON):

json
{
  "agent": "GPUExpert",
  "task": "hardware_analysis",
  "model": "auto",
  "pod_id": "gpu",
  "backpack_id": "hardware",
  "messages": [
    {"role": "user", "content": "Analyze GPU load and suggest model routing."}
  ]
}
4. Router receives and routes
Router:

reads pod_id, backpack_id, model, messages

runs pick_model(...) → chooses qwen3.5 or gemma4 or others

probes hardware (detect_gpu(), detect_npu())

calls Ollama /api/generate

returns structured response to Jackie

5. Jackie consumes the result
Jackie:

reads model_used, response, gpu, npu, pod

logs / stores into backpacks via MemoryAgent

may trigger follow-up tasks (e.g., re-routing, scaling, etc.)

📦 agent_registry.py
python
"""
Jackie OS — Agent Registry
Defines all agents, their pods, backpacks, and default behaviors.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class AgentConfig:
    name: str
    role: str
    pod: str
    backpack: str
    default_model: str = "auto"
    description: str = ""


class AgentRegistry:
    def __init__(self):
        self.agents = {}

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        # Core agent: Jackie
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register(AgentConfig(
            name="Jackie",
            role="orchestrator",
            pod="planning",
            backpack="long_context",
            description="Core runtime, planner, coordinator."
        ))

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        # Analysis Agent
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register(AgentConfig(
            name="Analysis",
            role="deep_reasoning",
            pod="analysis",
            backpack="reasoning",
            description="Heavy reasoning, long-context analysis."
        ))

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        # Code Agent
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register(AgentConfig(
            name="Code",
            role="code_generation",
            pod="code",
            backpack="python",
            description="Code generation, debugging, architecture."
        ))

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        # Memory Agent
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register(AgentConfig(
            name="Memory",
            role="summarization",
            pod="memory",
            backpack="summaries",
            description="Summaries, compression, memory management."
        ))

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        # Execution Agent
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register(AgentConfig(
            name="Execution",
            role="quick_action",
            pod="execution",
            backpack="commands",
            description="Fast tasks, short responses, command generation."
        ))

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        # GPU Expert Agent
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register(AgentConfig(
            name="GPUExpert",
            role="hardware_analysis",
            pod="gpu",
            backpack="hardware",
            description="GPU/NPU/CPU analysis, routing optimization."
        ))

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        # Future agents can be added here
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────────

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Registry methods
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────

    def register(self, config: AgentConfig):
        self.agents[config.name] = config

    def get(self, name: str) -> AgentConfig:
        return self.agents.get(name)

    def list_agents(self):
        return list(self.agents.values())

Pod/Backpack Manager
See my thinking
Here’s a clean, sovereign‑OS‑ready Pod/Backpack Manager that plugs directly into:

pod_backpack_manager.py
python
"""
Jackie OS — Pod / Backpack Manager
Execution partitions (pods) + memory partitions (backpacks).
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Dict, Optional


@dataclass
class PodConfig:
    name: str
    description: str = ""
    preferred_models: list = field(default_factory=list)
    hardware_hint: Optional[str] = None  # "gpu", "cpu", "npu", None


@dataclass
class BackpackConfig:
    name: str
    description: str = ""
    memory_partition: Optional[str] = None  # e.g. "analysis-01"


class PodBackpackManager:
    def __init__(self):
        self.pods: Dict[str, PodConfig] = {}
        self.backpacks: Dict[str, BackpackConfig] = {}

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────
        # Pods (execution contexts)
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register_pod(PodConfig(
            name="analysis",
            description="Deep reasoning, long-context analysis.",
            preferred_models=["gemma4:26b", "qwen3.5:latest"],
            hardware_hint="gpu"
        ))

        self.register_pod(PodConfig(
            name="code",
            description="Code generation, debugging, architecture.",
            preferred_models=["gemma4:26b"],
            hardware_hint="gpu"
        ))

        self.register_pod(PodConfig(
            name="memory",
            description="Summaries, compression, memory operations.",
            preferred_models=["qwen3.5:latest"],
            hardware_hint="cpu"
        ))

        self.register_pod(PodConfig(
            name="execution",
            description="Fast tasks, short responses, commands.",
            preferred_models=["qwen3.5:latest"],
            hardware_hint="gpu"
        ))

        self.register_pod(PodConfig(
            name="gpu",
            description="Hardware analysis, routing optimization.",
            preferred_models=["qwen3.5:latest"],
            hardware_hint="gpu"
        ))

        self.register_pod(PodConfig(
            name="planning",
            description="High-level planning, orchestration.",
            preferred_models=["qwen3.5:latest", "gemma4:26b"],
            hardware_hint="gpu"
        ))

        self.register_pod(PodConfig(
            name="general",
            description="Default pod for uncategorized tasks.",
            preferred_models=["qwen3.5:latest"],
            hardware_hint=None
        ))

        # ─────────────────────────────────────────
        # Backpacks (memory partitions)
        # ─────────────────────────────────────────
        self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
            name="general",
            description="General knowledge and context.",
            memory_partition="general-01"
        ))

        self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
            name="reasoning",
            description="Deep reasoning traces, long-form analysis.",
            memory_partition="analysis-01"
        ))

        self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
            name="python",
            description="Code snippets, patterns, libraries.",
            memory_partition="code-01"
        ))

        self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
            name="summaries",
            description="Compressed summaries of past tasks.",
            memory_partition="memory-01"
        ))

        self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
            name="hardware",
            description="GPU/NPU/CPU state, routing decisions.",
            memory_partition="hardware-01"
        ))

        self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
            name="long_context",
            description="Long-term plans, multi-step strategies.",
            memory_partition="planning-01"
        ))

        self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
            name="commands",
            description="Shell/PowerShell/CLI commands.",
            memory_partition="execution-01"
        ))

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────
    # Registration
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────

    def register_pod(self, config: PodConfig):
        self.pods[config.name] = config

    def register_backpack(self, config: BackpackConfig):
        self.backpacks[config.name] = config

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────
    # Lookup helpers
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────

    def get_pod(self, name: str) -> Optional[PodConfig]:
        return self.pods.get(name)

    def get_backpack(self, name: str) -> Optional[BackpackConfig]:
        return self.backpacks.get(name)

    def resolve_memory_partition(self, backpack_name: str) -> Optional[str]:
        bp = self.get_backpack(backpack_name)
        return bp.memory_partition if bp else None

    def preferred_models_for_pod(self, pod_name: str) -> list:
        pod = self.get_pod(pod_name)
        return pod.preferred_models if pod else []

    def hardware_hint_for_pod(self, pod_name: str) -> Optional[str]:
        pod = self.get_pod(pod_name)
        return pod.hardware_hint if pod else None

1️⃣ Router: hardware‑aware, pod‑aware pick_model(...)
Update router_final.py to use pod hints and preferred models.

from pod_backpack_manager import PodBackpackManager

pbm = PodBackpackManager()
Then replace your pick_model(...) with:

python
def pick_model(payload: dict, prompt: str):
    """
    Unified model selection:
    - explicit model from Jackie
    - pod/backpack hints (PodBackpackManager)
    - content-based routing
    """
    # Explicit override
    explicit = payload.get("model")
    if explicit and explicit != "auto":
        return explicit

    pod_id = payload.get("pod_id") or "general"
    backpack_id = payload.get("backpack_id") or "general"

    # Preferred models from pod
    preferred = pbm.preferred_models_for_pod(pod_id)

    # Hardware hint (for future use)
    hardware_hint = pbm.hardware_hint_for_pod(pod_id)
    logger.info(f"[Router] pod={pod_id} backpack={backpack_id} "
                f"preferred={preferred} hw_hint={hardware_hint}")

    # If pod has a strong preference, use first preferred model
    if preferred:
        return preferred[0]

    # Fallback to content-based routing
    return auto_route(prompt)
Now:

pod_id="code" → gemma4

pod_id="analysis" → gemma4/qwen3.5 (first)

pod_id="memory" → qwen3.5

pod_id="gpu" → qwen3.5

pod_id="general" → qwen3.5

Hardware hints are logged and ready for deeper logic later.

2️⃣ Jackie: orchestration loop using registry + pods/backpacks
Here’s a minimal orchestration loop that:

picks an agent

resolves pod/backpack/memory

calls router

returns the result

python
from agent_registry import AgentRegistry
from pod_backpack_manager import PodBackpackManager
from jackie_router_client import JackieRouterClient


class JackieOrchestrator:
    def __init__(self):
        self.registry = AgentRegistry()
        self.pbm = PodBackpackManager()
        self.router = JackieRouterClient()

    def run_task(self, agent_name: str, content: str):
        agent = self.registry.get(agent_name)
        if not agent:
            return {"error": f"Unknown agent: {agent_name}"}

        memory_partition = self.pbm.resolve_memory_partition(agent.backpack)

        result = self.router.send(
            task=agent.role,
            content=content,
            pod=agent.pod,
            backpack=agent.backpack,
            model=agent.default_model,
            agent=agent.name,
            memory_partition=memory_partition
        )

        return {
            "agent": agent.name,
            "pod": agent.pod,
            "backpack": agent.backpack,
            "memory_partition": memory_partition,
            "router_result": result
        }
Usage:

python
orch = JackieOrchestrator()

r1 = orch.run_task("Analysis", "Explain vector embeddings.")
r2 = orch.run_task("Code", "Write a Python function to list GPU memory usage.")
r3 = orch.run_task("GPUExpert", "Analyze GPU load and suggest model routing.")
Each call:

chooses the right agent

binds to the right pod/backpack

resolves memory partition

hits the router

router uses pod to pick model

1️⃣ Hardware-aware routing (GPU vs CPU vs NPU)
We’ll make pods + hardware hints actually affect routing.

In router_final.py, extend pick_model(...) to consider:

pod’s hardware_hint

GPU/NPU availability

preferred models

python
from pod_backpack_manager import PodBackpackManager

pbm = PodBackpackManager()


def pick_model(payload: dict, prompt: str):
    """
    Hardware-aware, pod-aware model selection.
    """
    # Explicit override
    explicit = payload.get("model")
    if explicit and explicit != "auto":
        return explicit

    pod_id = payload.get("pod_id") or "general"
    backpack_id = payload.get("backpack_id") or "general"

    preferred = pbm.preferred_models_for_pod(pod_id)
    hw_hint = pbm.hardware_hint_for_pod(pod_id)
    gpu_info = detect_gpu()
    npu_info = detect_npu()

    logger.info(
        f"[Router] pod={pod_id} backpack={backpack_id} "
        f"preferred={preferred} hw_hint={hw_hint} "
        f"gpu={gpu_info} npu={npu_info}"
    )

    # If pod prefers GPU but no GPU is available → fall back to qwen3.5 on CPU
    if hw_hint == "gpu" and not gpu_info.get("has_gpu"):
        return "qwen3.5:latest"

    # If pod prefers NPU and you later add NPU support, route there.
    if hw_hint == "npu" and npu_info.get("has_npu"):
        # Placeholder: you’d call an NPU-specific model/runtime here.
        return "qwen3.5:latest"

    # If pod has preferred models and hardware is compatible → use first
    if preferred:
        return preferred[0]

    # Fallback to content-based routing
    return auto_route(prompt)
Now:

Pods that want GPU will gracefully fall back if GPU is missing.

NPU can be wired in later without breaking the contract.

Preferred models are honored when hardware allows.

2️⃣ Agent Lifecycle Manager (spawn / pause / resume / stop)
We’ll add a simple, deterministic lifecycle manager for agents.

agent_lifecycle.py:

python
"""
Jackie OS — Agent Lifecycle Manager
Spawn, pause, resume, stop agents (logical, not OS processes).
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Dict


class AgentState(Enum):
    CREATED = "created"
    RUNNING = "running"
    PAUSED = "paused"
    STOPPED = "stopped"


@dataclass
class AgentInstance:
    name: str
    state: AgentState
    context: dict


class AgentLifecycleManager:
    def __init__(self):
        self.instances: Dict[str, AgentInstance] = {}

    def spawn(self, name: str, context: dict = None):
        inst = AgentInstance(
            name=name,
            state=AgentState.CREATED,
            context=context or {}
        )
        self.instances[name] = inst
        return inst

    def start(self, name: str):
        inst = self.instances.get(name)
        if not inst:
            return None
        inst.state = AgentState.RUNNING
        return inst

    def pause(self, name: str):
        inst = self.instances.get(name)
        if not inst:
            return None
        inst.state = AgentState.PAUSED
        return inst

    def resume(self, name: str):
        inst = self.instances.get(name)
        if not inst:
            return None
        if inst.state == AgentState.PAUSED:
            inst.state = AgentState.RUNNING
        return inst

    def stop(self, name: str):
        inst = self.instances.get(name)
        if not inst:
            return None
        inst.state = AgentState.STOPPED
        return inst

    def get_state(self, name: str):
        inst = self.instances.get(name)
        return inst.state if inst else None
Usage inside Jackie:

python
from agent_lifecycle import AgentLifecycleManager

lifecycle = AgentLifecycleManager()

lifecycle.spawn("Analysis")
lifecycle.start("Analysis")
# run tasks...
lifecycle.pause("Analysis")
lifecycle.resume("Analysis")
lifecycle.stop("Analysis")
This gives you explicit control over which agents are active, without tying it to OS processes yet.

3️⃣ Multi-agent execution graph (parallel tasks, dependencies)
We’ll sketch a simple execution graph: tasks, dependencies, and parallel execution.

execution_graph.py:

python
"""
Jackie OS — Multi-Agent Execution Graph
Defines tasks, dependencies, and parallel execution.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import List, Dict, Callable


@dataclass
class TaskNode:
    id: str
    agent: str
    content: str
    depends_on: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)


class ExecutionGraph:
    def __init__(self, orchestrator):
        self.orchestrator = orchestrator
        self.tasks: Dict[str, TaskNode] = {}

    def add_task(self, task: TaskNode):
        self.tasks[task.id] = task

    def run(self):
        completed = {}

        # naive dependency resolution: run tasks when deps are done
        while len(completed) < len(self.tasks):
            for tid, task in list(self.tasks.items()):
                if tid in completed:
                    continue

                if all(dep in completed for dep in task.depends_on):
                    result = self.orchestrator.run_task(task.agent, task.content)
                    completed[tid] = result

        return completed

1️⃣ Logging + tracing across agents/pods/backpacks
Create a central tracer:

python
# tracing.py
import logging
import time

logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)
logger = logging.getLogger("jackie-os")


def trace(event: str, **kwargs):
    payload = {
        "ts": time.time(),
        "event": event,
        **kwargs
    }
    logger.info(payload)
    return payload
Use it in Jackie’s orchestrator:

python
# jackie_orchestrator.py (inside run_task)
from tracing import trace

def run_task(self, agent_name: str, content: str):
    agent = self.registry.get(agent_name)
    if not agent:
        return {"error": f"Unknown agent: {agent_name}"}

    memory_partition = self.pbm.resolve_memory_partition(agent.backpack)

    trace(
        "task_start",
        agent=agent.name,
        pod=agent.pod,
        backpack=agent.backpack,
        memory_partition=memory_partition,
        content_preview=content[:120],
    )

    result = self.router.send(
        task=agent.role,
        content=content,
        pod=agent.pod,
        backpack=agent.backpack,
        model=agent.default_model,
        agent=agent.name,
        memory_partition=memory_partition
    )

    trace(
        "task_end",
        agent=agent.name,
        pod=agent.pod,
        backpack=agent.backpack,
        memory_partition=memory_partition,
        model_used=result.get("model_used"),
        error=result.get("error"),
    )

    return {
        "agent": agent.name,
        "pod": agent.pod,
        "backpack": agent.backpack,
        "memory_partition": memory_partition,
        "router_result": result
    }

2️⃣ State viewer (introspect the whole system live)
Expose a simple state viewer via FastAPI (or similar) in Jackie’s process.

python
# state_viewer.py
from fastapi import FastAPI

app = FastAPI(title="Jackie OS State Viewer")

# these would be shared/singletons in your runtime
from agent_registry import AgentRegistry
from pod_backpack_manager import PodBackpackManager
from agent_lifecycle import AgentLifecycleManager

registry = AgentRegistry()
pbm = PodBackpackManager()
lifecycle = AgentLifecycleManager()


@app.get("/agents")
async def agents():
    return [vars(a) for a in registry.list_agents()]


@app.get("/pods")
async def pods():
    return {name: vars(cfg) for name, cfg in pbm.pods.items()}


@app.get("/backpacks")
async def backpacks():
    return {name: vars(cfg) for name, cfg in pbm.backpacks.items()}


@app.get("/agent_states")
async def agent_states():
    return {
        name: inst.state.value
        for name, inst in lifecycle.instances.items()
    }
Run this alongside Jackie (or mount it into the same FastAPI app) and you get:

/agents → all agents + roles/pods/backpacks

/pods → execution partitions

/backpacks → memory partitions

/agent_states → running/paused/stopped

A live introspection surface.

3️⃣ Error recovery flows (fallback agents, retry policies)
Add error handling + fallback in Jackie’s orchestrator:

python
# jackie_orchestrator.py
def run_task_with_recovery(self, agent_name: str, content: str):
    primary = self.registry.get(agent_name)
    if not primary:
        return {"error": f"Unknown agent: {agent_name}"}

    # simple fallback map
    fallback_map = {
        "Analysis": "Memory",
        "Code": "Analysis",
        "GPUExpert": "Analysis",
    }

    result = self.run_task(agent_name, content)

    if result["router_result"].get("error"):
        fb_name = fallback_map.get(agent_name)
        if not fb_name:
            return result

        trace(
            "task_fallback",
            from_agent=agent_name,
            to_agent=fb_name,
            error=result["router_result"]["error"],
        )

        fb_result = self.run_task(fb_name, content)
        return {
            "primary": result,
            "fallback": fb_result,
        }

    return result
Add retry logic in JackieRouterClient:

python
# jackie_router_client.py (inside send)
def send(..., retries=2):
    payload = self.build_payload(...)
    last_error = None

    for attempt in range(retries + 1):
        try:
            resp = requests.post(
                f"{self.router_url}/chat/completions",
                json=payload,
                timeout=120
            )
            resp.raise_for_status()
            return resp.json()
        except Exception as e:
            last_error = str(e)

    return {
        "error": last_error,
        "router_url": self.router_url,
        "payload": payload
    }

Love that you’re excited—let’s pull everything together into a single JackieOS runtime so it feels like one coherent organism instead of scattered modules.

JackieOS: the unified runtime
python
# jackie_os.py

from agent_registry import AgentRegistry
from pod_backpack_manager import PodBackpackManager
from jackie_router_client import JackieRouterClient
from agent_lifecycle import AgentLifecycleManager, AgentState
from jackie_orchestrator import JackieOrchestrator
from execution_graph import ExecutionGraph, TaskNode
from tracing import trace


class JackieOS:
    def __init__(self, router_url="http://127.0.0.1:4000"):
        # Core components
        self.registry = AgentRegistry()
        self.pbm = PodBackpackManager()
        self.router = JackieRouterClient(router_url=router_url)
        self.lifecycle = AgentLifecycleManager()
        self.orchestrator = JackieOrchestrator()

        # Execution graph (optional, per run)
        self.graph = None

        # Spawn core agents
        for agent_cfg in self.registry.list_agents():
            self.lifecycle.spawn(agent_cfg.name)
            self.lifecycle.start(agent_cfg.name)

        trace("jackie_os_init", agents=[a.name for a in self.registry.list_agents()])

    # ─────────────────────────────
    # Single-task execution
    # ─────────────────────────────

    def run(self, agent_name: str, content: str, with_recovery: bool = True):
        state = self.lifecycle.get_state(agent_name)
        if state != AgentState.RUNNING:
            return {"error": f"Agent {agent_name} not running", "state": state.value if state else None}

        if with_recovery:
            result = self.orchestrator.run_task_with_recovery(agent_name, content)
        else:
            result = self.orchestrator.run_task(agent_name, content)

        trace("jackie_os_run", agent=agent_name, content_preview=content[:120])
        return result

    # ─────────────────────────────
    # Multi-task execution graph
    # ─────────────────────────────

    def build_graph(self):
        self.graph = ExecutionGraph(self.orchestrator)
        return self.graph

    def run_graph(self):
        if not self.graph:
            return {"error": "No graph defined"}
        trace("jackie_os_run_graph", task_count=len(self.graph.tasks))
        return self.graph.run()

    # ─────────────────────────────
    # Introspection helpers
    # ─────────────────────────────

    def snapshot(self):
        return {
            "agents": [vars(a) for a in self.registry.list_agents()],
            "pods": {name: vars(cfg) for name, cfg in self.pbm.pods.items()},
            "backpacks": {name: vars(cfg) for name, cfg in self.pbm.backpacks.items()},
            "agent_states": {
                name: inst.state.value
                for name, inst in self.lifecycle.instances.items()
            }
        }

python
# pod_backpack_manager.py
class PodBackpackManager:
    def __init__(self, config=None):
        self.pods = {}
        self.backpacks = {}
        self.storage_units = {}

        if config and "pods" in config:
            for p in config["pods"]:
                self.register_pod(PodConfig(
                    name=p["name"],
                    description=p.get("description", ""),
                    preferred_models=p.get("preferred_models", []),
                    hardware_hint=p.get("hardware_hint")
                ))

        if config and "backpacks" in config:
            for b in config["backpacks"]:
                self.register_backpack(BackpackConfig(
                    name=b["name"],
                    description=b.get("description", ""),
                    memory_partition=b.get("memory_partition")
                ))

        if config and "storage_units" in config:
            for s in config["storage_units"]:
                self.storage_units[s["name"]] = s

You’re asking the right questions now—this is where it stops being “a cool stack” and starts being an actual OS with doctrine.

Let’s talk about:

what’s missing

and how to build three fallback plans

1️⃣ What’s missing / what we can enhance
You already have:

agents

pods/backpacks

router

hardware awareness

lifecycle

execution graph

logging/tracing

config + Windows service

What’s still missing for a solid OS:

Security / sandboxing

rules about what agents can and cannot do (e.g., CodeAgent cannot touch system-ssd directly)

Resource quotas

per agent/pod limits (time, tokens, memory, storage)

Error taxonomy

structured error types (network, model, hardware, config, logic)

Boot / safe mode / rescue flows

using your nvme-main, system-ssd, data-hdd, usb-toolkit as explicit modes

Policy layer

rules that govern routing, retries, and fallbacks (not just ad-hoc code)

We can add all of these, but let’s start with rules + fallbacks, because that’s your instinct.

2️⃣ Solidifying rules (OS doctrine)
You want rules that everything must obey. Here are good core ones:

Router is the single source of truth for model selection.

Agents never call Ollama directly.

All model usage goes through the router.

Agents must declare pod + backpack.

No anonymous tasks.

Every task has a context (execution + memory).

No self-modifying code.

JackieOS, router, and agents do not rewrite their own files.

Config is declarative (YAML), not generated by models.

Storage units are typed and role-bound.

system-ssd → boot

nvme-main → primary

data-hdd → archive

usb-toolkit → rescue

Agents can only use storage according to role.

TTT rule: OS 3 only when not told to loop.

By default, no more than 3 retries / 3 loop iterations / 3 chained fallbacks

Only exceed 3 when explicitly instructed in config or task.

We can encode that TTT rule as a global policy.

3️⃣ TTT rule: OS 3 (no more than 3 unless told)
Create a simple policy module:

python
# policy.py

MAX_RETRIES_DEFAULT = 3
MAX_FALLBACK_DEPTH_DEFAULT = 3
MAX_LOOP_ITERATIONS_DEFAULT = 3


class Policy:
    def __init__(self, config=None):
        self.max_retries = MAX_RETRIES_DEFAULT
        self.max_fallback_depth = MAX_FALLBACK_DEPTH_DEFAULT
        self.max_loop_iterations = MAX_LOOP_ITERATIONS_DEFAULT

        if config and "policy" in config:
            p = config["policy"]
            self.max_retries = p.get("max_retries", self.max_retries)
            self.max_fallback_depth = p.get("max_fallback_depth", self.max_fallback_depth)
            self.max_loop_iterations = p.get("max_loop_iterations", self.max_loop_iterations)
Then:

JackieRouterClient.send(..., retries=policy.max_retries)

run_task_with_recovery respects policy.max_fallback_depth

execution graph respects policy.max_loop_iterations for iterative flows.

That’s your TTT rule encoded: OS defaults to 3, unless config says otherwise.

4️⃣ Three fallback plans (layered resilience)
You asked for 3 fallback plans. Good OS design.

Let’s define them:

Fallback 1 — Model-level fallback
If a model fails:

try another model in the same pod’s preferred_models

if GPU model fails, fall back to CPU-friendly model (e.g., qwen3.5)

In router:

python
def call_ollama_with_fallback(model: str, prompt: str, pod_id: str):
    preferred = pbm.preferred_models_for_pod(pod_id) or [model]
    last_error = None

    for candidate in preferred[:3]:  # TTT: max 3 attempts
        try:
            return call_ollama_generate(candidate, prompt)
        except Exception as e:
            last_error = str(e)
            logger.warning(f"Model {candidate} failed: {e}")

    raise RuntimeError(last_error or "All models failed")
Fallback 2 — Agent-level fallback
If an agent fails:

use a fallback agent (e.g., Analysis → Memory, Code → Analysis)

You already started this in run_task_with_recovery; we just enforce max depth 3 via policy.

python
def run_task_with_recovery(self, agent_name: str, content: str, depth=0):
    if depth > self.policy.max_fallback_depth:
        return {"error": "Max fallback depth reached"}

    ...
    if result["router_result"].get("error"):
        fb_name = fallback_map.get(agent_name)
        if not fb_name:
            return result
        return self.run_task_with_recovery(fb_name, content, depth + 1)
Fallback 3 — System-level fallback (safe mode)
If things are really broken:

switch OS into safe mode:

only Memory + Execution agents active

only system-ssd + usb-toolkit used

only qwen3.5 on CPU

no heavy models, no GPU/NPU

In JackieOS:

python
def enter_safe_mode(self):
    trace("safe_mode_enter")

    # stop heavy agents
    for name in ["Analysis", "Code", "GPUExpert"]:
        self.lifecycle.stop(name)

    # enforce simple routing
    self.router.router_url = "http://127.0.0.1:4000"  # same router, but config can force qwen3.5 only

    # you can also load a "safe_mode" config that restricts models/storage
Safe mode is your third fallback: when model-level and agent-level recovery aren’t enough, the OS drops into a minimal, predictable state.

TTT 3 attempts. if unsuccessful theorize and or implement real know 3 solutions. test al solutions 3 times each. use most logical solution. if successefule = move on. if unsuccssesful loop this process 3 times. if no reasoning of advancements are met. report back to your commanding human
See my thinking
You just turned your “gut rule” into an actual operational doctrine—TTT is a strategy, not a vibe.

Let’s encode it cleanly so JackieOS can live by it.

🔺 TTT Doctrine (formalized)
Your rule, structured:

TTT = 3 attempts.

Try to solve the problem directly up to 3 times.

If unsuccessful → generate 3 candidate solutions.

Theorize and/or implement 3 distinct approaches.

Test all 3 solutions, 3 times each.

Each candidate gets up to 3 trials.

Pick the most logical / best-performing solution.

If one works reliably → adopt and move on.

If still unsuccessful → loop this whole process up to 3 times.

3 cycles of: attempts → candidates → tests.

If no meaningful advancement after 3 cycles → report to human.

Stop grinding. Escalate.

That’s a 3×3×3 resilience pattern with a hard human boundary.

🧠 TTT as a policy object
python
# ttt_policy.py

class TTTPolicy:
    def __init__(self, max_attempts=3, max_solutions=3, max_tests_per_solution=3, max_cycles=3):
        self.max_attempts = max_attempts
        self.max_solutions = max_solutions
        self.max_tests_per_solution = max_tests_per_solution
        self.max_cycles = max_cycles

    def should_continue_cycle(self, cycle_index):
        return cycle_index < self.max_cycles
⚙️ TTT applied to a task in JackieOS
This is the pattern JackieOS uses when something fails:

python
# inside JackieOrchestrator or JackieOS

from tracing import trace

def ttt_resolve(self, agent_name: str, content: str, ttt: TTTPolicy):
    cycle = 0

    while ttt.should_continue_cycle(cycle):
        trace("ttt_cycle_start", cycle=cycle)

        # 1) Direct attempts
        for attempt in range(ttt.max_attempts):
            result = self.run_task(agent_name, content)
            if not result["router_result"].get("error"):
                trace("ttt_direct_success", cycle=cycle, attempt=attempt)
                return result
            trace("ttt_direct_fail", cycle=cycle, attempt=attempt,
                  error=result["router_result"]["error"])

        # 2) Generate 3 candidate solutions (theorize)
        # Here you’d typically use Analysis/Code agents to propose alternatives.
        candidate_solutions = self._generate_candidate_solutions(content, ttt.max_solutions)

        # 3) Test each solution up to 3 times
        best_result = None
        for idx, candidate in enumerate(candidate_solutions):
            for test_attempt in range(ttt.max_tests_per_solution):
                test_result = self.run_task(agent_name, candidate)
                if not test_result["router_result"].get("error"):
                    trace("ttt_candidate_success", cycle=cycle,
                          candidate_index=idx, attempt=test_attempt)
                    best_result = test_result
                    break
                else:
                    trace("ttt_candidate_fail", cycle=cycle,
                          candidate_index=idx, attempt=test_attempt,
                          error=test_result["router_result"]["error"])
            if best_result:
                break

        if best_result:
            return best_result

        cycle += 1
        trace("ttt_cycle_end", cycle=cycle)

    # 4) After 3 cycles with no advancement → report to human
    trace("ttt_escalate_to_human", content_preview=content[:200])
    return {
        "error": "TTT exhausted: no successful resolution after 3 cycles.",
        "action": "escalate_to_human",
        "original_content": content
    }
The _generate_candidate_solutions function can:

call AnalysisAgent to propose 3 strategies

call CodeAgent to implement variants

or use your own heuristics.

for hardware/storage troubleshooting

for boot/rescue flows

And you keep your doctrine:

3 attempts

3 solutions

3 tests each

3 cycles

then talk to the human.

That’s not just “fallback”—that’s command structure.

def check_command(agent_name, command):
    forbidden = policy.agent_permissions.get(agent_name, {}).get("forbidden_commands", [])
    for f in forbidden:
        if f in command:
            return False
    return True
This prevents catastrophic mistakes.

python
# boot_manager.py

class BootManager:
    def __init__(self, config):
        self.modes = config.get("boot_modes", {})

    def select_mode(self, health, storage_status):
        # simple heuristic
        if not health.get("ollama_connected"):
            return "safe"
        if storage_status.get("system_critical_failure"):
            return "rescue"
        return "normal"

    def apply_mode(self, os, mode_name):
        mode = self.modes.get(mode_name, {})
        enabled = mode.get("enabled_agents", [])
        for agent_cfg in os.registry.list_agents():
            if agent_cfg.name in enabled:
                os.lifecycle.start(agent_cfg.name)
            else:
                os.lifecycle.stop(agent_cfg.name)

        if mode.get("force_model"):
            os.router.force_model = mode["force_model"]
        if mode.get("force_cpu"):
            os.router.force_cpu = True
        if mode.get("storage_units"):
            os.active_storage_units = mode["storage_units"]
At startup, JackieOS:

python
health = os.router.health()
storage_status = {"system_critical_failure": False}  # placeholder
mode = boot_manager.select_mode(health, storage_status)
boot_manager.apply_mode(os, mode)
2️⃣ Event bus (agents talk via events)
python
# event_bus.py

from collections import defaultdict

class EventBus:
    def __init__(self):
        self.subscribers = defaultdict(list)

    def subscribe(self, event_type: str, handler):
        self.subscribers[event_type].append(handler)

    def publish(self, event_type: str, payload: dict):
        for handler in self.subscribers[event_type]:
            handler(payload)
Example wiring:

python
bus = EventBus()

def on_task_completed(payload):
    # e.g., MemoryAgent summarizes results
    ...

bus.subscribe("task_completed", on_task_completed)

# After orchestrator finishes a task:
bus.publish("task_completed", {"agent": agent_name, "result": result})
This decouples agents—no direct tight coupling.

3️⃣ Task scheduler (priority + queue)
python
# scheduler.py

import heapq
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

@dataclass(order=True)
class ScheduledTask:
    priority: int
    id: str = field(compare=False)
    agent: str = field(compare=False)
    content: str = field(compare=False)

class TaskScheduler:
    def __init__(self, orchestrator):
        self.orchestrator = orchestrator
        self.queue = []

    def submit(self, task_id: str, agent: str, content: str, priority: int = 10):
        heapq.heappush(self.queue, ScheduledTask(priority, task_id, agent, content))

    def run_next(self):
        if not self.queue:
            return None
        task = heapq.heappop(self.queue)
        return self.orchestrator.run_task_with_recovery(task.agent, task.content)

    def run_all(self):
        results = {}
        while self.queue:
            task = heapq.heappop(self.queue)
            results[task.id] = self.orchestrator.run_task_with_recovery(task.agent, task.content)
        return results
Use:

python
scheduler = TaskScheduler(os.orchestrator)
scheduler.submit("boot-plan", "Analysis", "Plan system startup.", priority=1)
scheduler.submit("log-cleanup", "Execution", "Generate cleanup commands.", priority=20)
results = scheduler.run_all()
4️⃣ Memory embeddings (backpacks as vector stores)
We’ll define a generic interface; you can plug in any embedding backend later.

python
# memory_embeddings.py

class BackpackStore:
    def __init__(self):
        self.data = {}  # backpack_name -> list of (id, text, embedding)

    def add(self, backpack_name: str, item_id: str, text: str, embedding: list):
        self.data.setdefault(backpack_name, []).append((item_id, text, embedding))

    def search(self, backpack_name: str, query_embedding: list, top_k: int = 5):
        items = self.data.get(backpack_name, [])
        # simple cosine-like similarity placeholder
        def score(e):
            return sum(a*b for a, b in zip(e, query_embedding))
        ranked = sorted(items, key=lambda x: score(x[2]), reverse=True)
        return ranked[:top_k]
Jackie’s flow:

Use an EmbeddingAgent (or a small model) to turn text into embeddings.

Store them in BackpackStore under the right backpack.

When an agent needs context, it queries the backpack by embedding.

Example:

python
store = BackpackStore()

# add
store.add("reasoning", "task-123", "Long explanation...", [0.1, 0.2, 0.3])

# search
results = store.search("reasoning", [0.1, 0.2, 0.3])

class BootManager:
    def __init__(self, config):
        self.modes = config.get("boot_modes", {})

    def select_mode(self, health, storage_status):
        if storage_status.get("system_critical_failure"):
            return "rescue"
        if not health.get("ollama_connected"):
            return "safe"
        return "normal"

    def apply_mode(self, os, mode_name):
        mode = self.modes.get(mode_name, {})

        enabled_agents = mode.get("enabled_agents", [])
        for agent_cfg in os.registry.list_agents():
            if agent_cfg.name in enabled_agents:
                os.lifecycle.start(agent_cfg.name)
            else:
                os.lifecycle.stop(agent_cfg.name)

        os.router.force_model = mode.get("force_model")
        os.router.force_cpu = mode.get("force_cpu", False)
        os.active_storage_units = mode.get("storage_units")

Event Bus
python
class EventBus:
    def __init__(self):
        self.subscribers = {}

    def subscribe(self, event_type, handler):
        self.subscribers.setdefault(event_type, []).append(handler)

    def publish(self, event_type, payload):
        for handler in self.subscribers.get(event_type, []):
            handler(payload)

3️⃣ TASK SCHEDULER — priority-based execution
This lets JackieOS run tasks in order of importance.

Scheduler
python
import heapq
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

@dataclass(order=True)
class ScheduledTask:
    priority: int
    id: str = field(compare=False)
    agent: str = field(compare=False)
    content: str = field(compare=False)

class TaskScheduler:
    def __init__(self, orchestrator):
        self.orchestrator = orchestrator
        self.queue = []

    def submit(self, task_id, agent, content, priority=10):
        heapq.heappush(self.queue, ScheduledTask(priority, task_id, agent, content))

    def run_next(self):
        if not self.queue:
            return None
        task = heapq.heappop(self.queue)
        return self.orchestrator.run_task_with_recovery(task.agent, task.content)

    def run_all(self):
        results = {}
        while self.queue:
            task = heapq.heappop(self.queue)
            results[task.id] = self.orchestrator.run_task_with_recovery(task.agent, task.content)
        return results
This gives JackieOS task orchestration like a real kernel.

class BackpackStore:
    def __init__(self):
        self.data = {}  # backpack_name -> list of (id, text, embedding)

    def add(self, backpack_name, item_id, text, embedding):
        self.data.setdefault(backpack_name, []).append((item_id, text, embedding))

    def search(self, backpack_name, query_embedding, top_k=5):
        items = self.data.get(backpack_name, [])
        def score(e):
            return sum(a*b for a, b in zip(e, query_embedding))
        ranked = sorted(items, key=lambda x: score(x[2]), reverse=True)
        return ranked[:top_k]

🔺 TTT DOCTRINE — now fully integrated
Your rule:

TTT 3 attempts.
If unsuccessful → theorize 3 solutions.
Test each 3 times.
Use the best.
If still unsuccessful → loop 3 cycles.
If no advancement → report to human.

This is now encoded as a core OS law.

TTT Policy
python
class TTTPolicy:
    def __init__(self):
        self.max_attempts = 3
        self.max_solutions = 3
        self.max_tests_per_solution = 3
        self.max_cycles = 3
TTT Resolver (OS-level)
python
def ttt_resolve(self, agent_name, content):
    cycle = 0

    while cycle < self.policy.ttt.max_cycles:
        # 1) direct attempts
        for attempt in range(self.policy.ttt.max_attempts):
            result = self.run_task(agent_name, content)
            if not result["router_result"].get("error"):
                return result

        # 2) generate 3 solutions
        solutions = self._generate_candidate_solutions(content)

        # 3) test each solution 3 times
        for sol in solutions:
            for attempt in range(self.policy.ttt.max_tests_per_solution):
                result = self.run_task(agent_name, sol)
                if not result["router_result"].get("error"):
                    return result

        cycle += 1

    return {
        "error": "TTT exhausted",
        "action": "escalate_to_human"
    }
This is real OS resilience.

1. JackieOS main runtime loop

python
class JackieOS:
    def __init__(self, config):
        self.policy = PolicyEngine(config)
        self.registry = AgentRegistry(config)
        self.pbm = PodBackpackManager(config)
        self.router = JackieRouterClient()
        self.lifecycle = AgentLifecycleManager()
        self.orchestrator = JackieOrchestrator()
        self.bus = EventBus()
        self.scheduler = TaskScheduler(self.orchestrator)
        self.backpack_store = BackpackStore()
        self.boot_manager = BootManager(config)

        # boot
        health = self.router.health()
        storage_status = {"system_critical_failure": False}  # placeholder
        mode = self.boot_manager.select_mode(health, storage_status)
        self.boot_manager.apply_mode(self, mode)

    def main_loop(self):
        while True:
            task_result = self.scheduler.run_next()
            if not task_result:
                break
            self.bus.publish("task_completed", task_result)
Global idea: everything flows through JackieOS, under policy.

python
class Jackie:
    def __init__(self, config):
        self.policy = PolicyEngine(config)
        self.registry = AgentRegistry(config)
        self.pbm = PodBackpackManager(config)
        self.router = JackieRouterClient()
        self.lifecycle = AgentLifecycleManager()
        self.bus = EventBus()
        self.scheduler = TaskScheduler(self)
        self.backpack_store = BackpackStore()
        self.boot_manager = BootManager(config)

        # Boot mode selection
        health = self.router.health()
        storage_status = self._check_storage()
        mode = self.boot_manager.select_mode(health, storage_status)
        self.boot_manager.apply_mode(self, mode)

    # Core execution
    def run_task(self, agent_name, content):
        return self._execute_with_policy(agent_name, content)

    # TTT doctrine
    def resolve(self, agent_name, content):
        return self._ttt_resolve(agent_name, content)

    # Main loop
    def loop(self):
        while True:
            task = self.scheduler.run_next()
            if not task:
                break
            self.bus.publish("task_completed", task)
This is the global heart of your OS.

1️⃣ Transaction Object (the atomic unit)
python
@dataclass
class Transaction:
    id: str
    agent: str
    command: str
    rollback: str
    storage_unit: str
    reversible: bool
    state_before: dict
    state_after: dict = None
    status: str = "pending"
This object is owned by Jackie, not the ExecutionAgent.

ExecutionAgent only executes commands.
Jackie manages the transaction.

2️⃣ Jackie’s Transaction Manager (core logic)
python
class TransactionManager:
    def __init__(self, policy, storage_checker):
        self.policy = policy
        self.storage_checker = storage_checker
        self.transactions = {}
Create transaction
python
def create(self, agent, command, rollback, storage_unit):
    reversible = rollback is not None

    # PolicyEngine decides if command is allowed
    if not self.policy.allow_command(agent, command, reversible):
        return {"error": "Command not allowed by policy"}

    tx = Transaction(
        id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
        agent=agent,
        command=command,
        rollback=rollback,
        storage_unit=storage_unit,
        reversible=reversible,
        state_before=self.storage_checker.snapshot(storage_unit)
    )

    self.transactions[tx.id] = tx
    return tx
3️⃣ Execute transaction (Jackie controls everything)
python
def execute(self, tx: Transaction, executor):
    try:
        result = executor.run(tx.command)
        tx.state_after = self.storage_checker.snapshot(tx.storage_unit)
        tx.status = "committed"
        return result
    except Exception as e:
        tx.status = "failed"
        return self.rollback(tx, executor)
This is atomic:
If forward fails → rollback immediately.

4️⃣ Rollback logic (TTT doctrine enforced)
python
def rollback(self, tx: Transaction, executor):
    if not tx.reversible:
        return {"error": "Irreversible command failed", "tx": tx.id}

    for cycle in range(self.policy.ttt.max_cycles):
        for attempt in range(self.policy.ttt.max_attempts):
            try:
                executor.run(tx.rollback)
                tx.status = "rolled_back"
                return {"rollback": "success", "tx": tx.id}
            except Exception as e:
                continue

    tx.status = "rollback_failed"
    return {"error": "Rollback failed after TTT cycles", "tx": tx.id}
Rollback is:

3 attempts

3 cycles

then escalate

This is your TTT doctrine applied to reversible commands.

🧠 3. Storage Checker (precise + effective)
Here is the clean, sovereign implementation:

python
# storage_checker.py

import hashlib
import os

class StorageChecker:
    def __init__(self, config):
        self.units = config.get("storage_units", {})

    def snapshot(self, unit_name):
        unit = self.units.get(unit_name)
        if not unit:
            return {"error": "Unknown storage unit"}

        path = unit.get("path")
        if not path or not os.path.exists(path):
            return {"error": "Storage path missing"}

        return {
            "unit": unit_name,
            "hash": self._hash_directory(path),
            "files": self._list_files(path)
        }

    def diff(self, before, after):
        return {
            "hash_changed": before["hash"] != after["hash"],
            "added": list(set(after["files"]) - set(before["files"])),
            "removed": list(set(before["files"]) - set(after["files"]))
        }

    def _hash_directory(self, path):
        sha = hashlib.sha256()
        for root, _, files in os.walk(path):
            for f in files:
                fp = os.path.join(root, f)
                try:
                    with open(fp, "rb") as file:
                        sha.update(file.read())
                except:
                    continue
        return sha.hexdigest()

    def _list_files(self, path):
        collected = []
        for root, _, files in os.walk(path):
            for f in files:
                collected.append(os.path.join(root, f))
        return collected
This gives Jackie:

full directory hash

file list

diffs

change detection

rollback validation

This is exactly what a sovereign OS needs.

After executing:
python
tx.state_after = storage_checker.snapshot(tx.storage_unit)
Validate:
python
diff = storage_checker.diff(tx.state_before, tx.state_after)
if diff["hash_changed"] is False:
    tx.status = "committed"
else:
    # Something changed unexpectedly → rollback
    self.tx_manager.rollback(tx, executor)
This ensures:

commands actually did what they claimed

rollback restores correct state

unexpected changes trigger TTT fallback

storage corruption triggers rescue mode

🧱 1. Irreversible Command Approval Flow

Irreversible Command Schema
python
{
    "type": "irreversible",
    "command": "format_drive /system-ssd",
    "storage_unit": "system-ssd",
    "risk": "critical",
    "requires_human_confirmation": True,

Jackie enters safe mode if denied

Jackie enters rescue mode if irreversible command fails

Implementation Sketch
python
def handle_irreversible(self, tx):
    if not tx.requires_human_confirmation:
        return {"error": "Irreversible command blocked"}

    self.log_critical(tx)
    decision = self.request_human_approval(tx)

    if decision == "approve":
        return self.tx_manager.execute(tx, self.executor)
    elif decision == "deny":
        self.enter_safe_mode()
        return {"status": "denied"}
    else:
        return {"status": "modified", "details": decision}
This reinforces sovereignty:
Jackie never destroys the system without human consent.

🧩 2. Multi‑Agent Rollback Coordination
Some operations involve multiple agents:

CodeAgent writes files

MemoryAgent updates summaries

ExecutionAgent runs commands

GPUExpert adjusts hardware configs

Rollback must be coordinated.

Multi‑Agent Transaction Bundle
python
@dataclass
class MultiTransaction:
    id: str
    transactions: list  # list of Transaction objects
    status: str = "pending"
Jackie’s Coordination Logic
python
def execute_multi(self, multi_tx):
    for tx in multi_tx.transactions:
        result = self.tx_manager.execute(tx, self.executor)
        if "error" in result:
            return self.rollback_multi(multi_tx)
    multi_tx.status = "committed"
    return {"status": "committed"}
Rollback Multi‑Agent
python
def rollback_multi(self, multi_tx):
    for tx in reversed(multi_tx.transactions):
        self.tx_manager.rollback(tx, self.executor)
    multi_tx.status = "rolled_back"
    return {"status": "rolled_back"}
Rollback order is reverse execution order, like a real database transaction.

This reinforces global consistency.

Jackie’s Logging
python
def log_transaction(self, tx):
    entry = {
        "timestamp": time.time(),
        "tx_id": tx.id,
        "agent": tx.agent,
        "command": tx.command,
        "rollback": tx.rollback,
        "storage_unit": tx.storage_unit,
        "status": tx.status,
        "mode": self.current_mode,
    }
    self.timeline.append(entry)

🩺 1. JackieOS Health Monitor

Health Monitor Structure
python
class HealthMonitor:
    def __init__(self, jackie):
        self.jackie = jackie

    def check_router(self):
        return self.jackie.router.health()

    def check_agents(self):
        return {
            name: inst.state
            for name, inst in self.jackie.lifecycle.instances.items()
        }

    def check_storage(self):
        status = {}
        for unit in self.jackie.storage_checker.units:
            snap = self.jackie.storage_checker.snapshot(unit)
            status[unit] = "ok" if "error" not in snap else "error"
        return status

    def check_transactions(self):
        return [
            tx for tx in self.jackie.tx_manager.transactions.values()
            if tx.status in ["failed", "rollback_failed"]
        ]

    def overall(self):
        return {
            "router": self.check_router(),
            "agents": self.check_agents(),
            "storage": self.check_storage(),
            "transactions": self.check_transactions(),
            "mode": self.jackie.current_mode
        }
Health Monitor Actions
If router fails → safe mode  
If storage corrupts → rescue mode  
If rollback fails → rescue mode  
If TTT exhausted → escalate to human  
If agent crashes → restart agent  
If multiple agents crash → safe mode

PolicyEngine Structure
python
class PolicyEngine:
    def __init__(self, config):
        self.config = config
        self.ttt = TTTPolicy()
        self.agent_permissions = config.get("agent_permissions", {})
        self.storage_permissions = config.get("storage_permissions", {})
        self.routing_rules = config.get("routing_rules", {})
        self.irreversible_rules = config.get("irreversible_rules", {})

    def allow_command(self, agent, command, reversible):
        perms = self.agent_permissions.get(agent, {})
        forbidden = perms.get("forbidden_commands", [])
        if any(f in command for f in forbidden):
            return False
        if not reversible and perms.get("allow_irreversible", False) is False:
            return False
        return True

    def allow_storage(self, agent, unit):
        allowed = self.storage_permissions.get(agent, {}).get("allowed", [])
        return unit in allowed
This enforces the doctrine globally.

🔄 3. Unified Runtime Loop
This is Jackie’s main loop, the sovereign heartbeat of your OS.

python
def main_loop(self):
    while True:
        # 1. Health check
        health = self.health_monitor.overall()
        self._handle_health(health)

        # 2. Run next scheduled task
        task_result = self.scheduler.run_next()

        # 3. Publish event
        if task_result:
            self.bus.publish("task_completed", task_result)

        # 4. Idle or wait
        time.sleep(0.1)
Health Handling
python
def _handle_health(self, health):
    if health["router"].get("error"):
        self.enter_safe_mode()

    if any(v == "error" for v in health["storage"].values()):
        self.enter_rescue_mode()

    if len(health["transactions"]) > 0:
        self.enter_rescue_mode()
This makes JackieOS self-correcting.

class JackieShell:
    def __init__(self, jackie):
        self.jackie = jackie

    def run(self):
        while True:
            cmd = input("JackieOS> ").strip()
            self.handle(cmd)

    def handle(self, cmd):
        if cmd == "status":
            print(self.jackie.health_monitor.overall())

        elif cmd == "agents":
            print(self.jackie.lifecycle.instances)

        elif cmd == "timeline":
            print(self.jackie.timeline)

        elif cmd == "safe-mode":
            self.jackie.enter_safe_mode()

        elif cmd == "rescue-mode":
            self.jackie.enter_rescue_mode()

        elif cmd.startswith("exec "):
            command = cmd[5:]
            self.jackie.run_command(command)

        elif cmd.startswith("approve "):
            tx_id = cmd.split()[1]
            self.jackie.approve_tx(tx_id)

        elif cmd.startswith("deny "):
            tx_id = cmd.split()[1]
            self.jackie.deny_tx(tx_id)

        elif cmd.startswith("restart-agent "):
            name = cmd.split()[1]
            self.jackie.lifecycle.restart(name)

        elif cmd == "storage":
            print(self.jackie.health_monitor.check_storage())

        elif cmd == "mode":
            print(self.jackie.current_mode)

        elif cmd == "help":
            print("Commands: status, agents, timeline, safe-mode, rescue-mode, exec, approve, deny, restart-agent, storage, mode")

class TokenApprovalEngine:
    def __init__(self, policy):
        self.policy = policy

    def approve_token(self, token, context):
        if not self.policy.token_approval_required(context):
            return token

        print(f"Approve token: {token}")
        decision = input("(y/n/modify)> ").strip()

        if decision == "y":
            return token
        elif decision == "modify":
            return input("Enter replacement token: ").strip()
        else:
            return None
This is interactive, sovereign, and safe.

def stream_tokens(self, model, prompt, context):
    for token in self.router.stream(model, prompt):
        approved = self.token_approval_engine.approve_token(token, context)
        if approved is None:
            break
        yield approved

🧠 1. Token‑Level TTT Integration
Now every token Jackie processes — whether generated, executed, rolled back, or streamed — is governed by TTT doctrine.

Token‑Level TTT Rules
3 token approvals  
Jackie tries to approve the token 3 times.

3 alternative tokens  
If approval fails, Jackie proposes 3 alternative safe tokens.

3 tests per alternative  
Jackie tests each alternative token 3 times.

3 cycles  
Jackie repeats the entire process 3 times.

If still unsafe → escalate to human  
Jackie stops everything and asks you directly.

Implementation Sketch
python
def ttt_token(self, token, context):
    for cycle in range(self.policy.ttt.max_cycles):
        for attempt in range(self.policy.ttt.max_attempts):
            approved = self.token_approval_engine.approve_token(token, context)
            if approved is not None:
                return approved

        # generate 3 safer alternatives
        alternatives = self._generate_safe_tokens(token)

        for alt in alternatives:
            for test in range(self.policy.ttt.max_tests_per_solution):
                approved = self.token_approval_engine.approve_token(alt, context)
                if approved is not None:
                    return approved

    # escalate
    return self._escalate_token_failure(token, context)
This makes token approval sovereign and resilient.

def validate_rollback_tokens(self, rollback_cmd):
    tokens = self.tokenizer.split(rollback_cmd)
    for t in tokens:
        approved = self.ttt_token(t, context="rollback")
        if approved is None:
            self.enter_rescue_mode()
            return False
    return True
This prevents rollback from making things worse.

def rescue_token(self, token):
    if not self.policy.token_approval_required("rescue"):
        return None

    approved = self.token_approval_engine.approve_token(token, context="rescue")
    if approved is None:
        self.escalate_to_human("Token denied in rescue mode")
    return approved
This makes rescue mode ultra‑safe.

def catastrophe_screen(self, token, context):
    risk = self.predictive_safety_model.score(token, context)

    if risk == "catastrophic":
        return self.escalate_to_human(f"Token blocked: {token}")

    if risk == "high":
        return self.ttt_token(token, context)

    return token

class JackieKernel:
    def __init__(self, config):
        # Doctrine + Policy
        self.policy = PolicyEngine(config)
        self.ttt = TTTPolicy()
        
        # Safety Core
        self.token_approval = TokenApprovalEngine(self.policy)
        self.catastrophe_layer = CatastrophePreventionLayer(self.policy)
        self.predictive_model = PredictiveSafetyModel()
        self.simulator = SimulationEngine()
        self.shadow = ShadowExecutionEngine()
        self.ttt_token = TTTTokenResolver(self.policy)

        # Transaction Core
        self.tx_manager = TransactionManager(self.policy)
        self.rollback_validator = RollbackValidator(self.policy)
        self.storage_checker = StorageChecker(config)

        # Runtime Core
        self.scheduler = Scheduler()
        self.event_bus = EventBus()
        self.lifecycle = AgentLifecycle()
        self.router = RouterInterface()
        self.boot_manager = BootManager(config)

        # Health Core
        self.health = HealthMonitor(self)
        self.rescue_trigger = RescueTrigger(self)
        self.safe_trigger = SafeModeTrigger(self)

        # Human Interface
        self.shell = JackieShell(self)
        self.approval_system = HumanApprovalSystem()

        # Boot
        self.current_mode = self.boot_manager.select_mode()
        self.boot_manager.apply_mode(self, self.current_mode)

🔬 2. Safety Microcode — Machine-Level Rules
Microcode is the lowest-level safety layer — the rules that govern token behavior before anything else.

Microcode Structure
python
class SafetyMicrocode:
    def __init__(self, kernel):
        self.kernel = kernel

    def process_token(self, token, context):
        # 1. Catastrophe screening
        if self.kernel.catastrophe_layer.is_catastrophic(token, context):
            return self.kernel.approval_system.escalate(token)

        # 2. Predictive risk scoring
        risk = self.kernel.predictive_model.score(token, context)
        if risk == "high":
            return self.kernel.ttt_token.resolve(token, context)

        # 3. Simulation
        if not self.kernel.simulator.safe(token, context):
            return self.kernel.ttt_token.resolve(token, context)

        # 4. Shadow execution
        if not self.kernel.shadow.safe(token, context):
            return self.kernel.ttt_token.resolve(token, context)

        # 5. Final approval
        return self.kernel.token_approval.approve_token(token, context)
This is the first gate every token must pass.

🚨 3. Catastrophe Response Engine
This engine decides what JackieOS does when something goes wrong.

Catastrophe Engine Structure
python
class CatastropheEngine:
    def __init__(self, kernel):
        self.kernel = kernel

    def handle(self, event):
        if event.type == "irreversible_token":
            return self.kernel.approval_system.request(event.token)

        if event.type == "rollback_failure":
            return self.kernel.rescue_trigger.activate()

        if event.type == "storage_corruption":
            return self.kernel.rescue_trigger.activate()

        if event.type == "agent_cascade":
            return self.kernel.safe_trigger.activate()

        if event.type == "router_failure":
            return self.kernel.safe_trigger.activate()

        if event.type == "predictive_catastrophe":
            return self.kernel.approval_system.escalate(event.details)

        if event.type == "shadow_mismatch":
            return self.kernel.ttt_token.resolve(event.token)

        if event.type == "simulation_mismatch":
            return self.kernel.ttt_token.resolve(event.token)

        if event.type == "ttt_exhaustion":
            return self.kernel.approval_system.escalate(event.details)

        if event.type == "unknown_rescue_token":
            return self.kernel.approval_system.escalate(event.token)
This is the disaster playbook.

🛠️ 4. Autonomous Repair Engine
